市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

不另行通知。
擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

CHPT
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and the structural shift towards high-end
testing. Our target price of NT$4,350 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the company's average forward PE
since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical

integration model. With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play
(rather than a consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin

compression from lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated US AI GPU and ASIC programs;
3) intensifying competition in testing boards from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Idea
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Momentum Seen Sustained into
2027
SL T, photonics (transceiver+CPO), and power testing should
continue to drive growth into 2027, with contributions from FT
handlers and burn-in ovens likely emerging as well. Stay OW.
3Q26 miss on GM: See details in Results Summary
Growth momentum seen sustained into 4Q26 and 2027: Management indicated
that the strong business momentum observed in 3Q26 is likely to stay intact going

forward, driven by both power testing and semi & photonics businesses. Normalized
GM remains at ~60% (2026 guidance of ~60% GM implies GM back to ~60% in
4Q26 vs. 57.5% in 3Q26).
• Power testing: 60-70% of revenue is AI-related for 2026, and it is expected
to be sustained into 2027. HVDC testing tools could be the major driver, and
it has already received orders from customers.
• SLT: 9M26 revenue doubled on a YoY basis. SL T demand used to focus on

one major customer, but management indicated that it has added AVGO and
MRVL as new customers. Moreover, demand in 4Q26/1H27 should be mostly
related to inference applications, including CPU/ASIC/LPU.
• Photonics: 9M26 revenue grew by 4x on  a YoY basis. Demand from
transceivers should remain solid and CPO should grow notably in 2027.
Chroma focuses on Insertion 3/4o and shipments for the mass production
line next year  are seen reaching 4-5x vs. that for the pilot line this year.

Moreover, it has added another customer for a pilot run in 9M27 and mass
production in 4Q27. At the industry level, if insertion is excluded from mass
production, Insertion 3 tool demand should be higher. Opportunities will
likely be much larger when CPO penetrates the scale-up network.
• New businesses: FT handlers have been qualified by multiple HPC/CSP
customers and orders have been placed. Burn-in ovens have secured the first
CSP customer, with more HPC customers expected to follow next year.

These should be new addressable markets for Chroma in the coming years.
Stay OW: We believe Chroma should continue to benefit from the AI compute/
networking/power testing opportunities due to its strong customer relationship,
solid execution and outstanding technology expertise. Our PT of NT$2,800 is based
on 40x 2027e P/E, implying a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

3Q26 miss on GM:
Off revenue of  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY), GM contracted 3ppt QoQ, to
57.5%, below our estimate at 60.5% and consensus at 62.7%, mainly owing to a less
favorable product mix.
Opex of NT$3,141mn (opex ratio 17.5%) was lower than our estimate at NT$3,797mn (opex
ratio 21.2%) and consensus at NT$3,321mn (opex ratio 21.7%), reflecting operating
leverage.
This led to operating profit of NT$7,167mn (OPM up 1.2ppt QoQ, to 40.0%), 2% higher

than our estimate at NT$7,040mn (OPM 39.3%) and 14% higher than consensus at NT
$6,281mn (OPM 41.0%).
With a narrower non-op gain of NT$186mn, net profit reached NT$5,647mn (EPS NT
$13.37), 2% short of our estimate at NT$5,790mn (EPS NT$13.71) and 6% short of
consensus at NT$5,317mn (EPS NT$12.59).

A Closer Look
Test instrument and ATS segment looks set to reach another
record high in 2026
Revenue for the segment increased 55% YoY to NT$10,546mn (37% of the mix) in 2025.
We estimate it will rise 118% YoY to NT$24,564mn (40% of the mix) in 2026. The ever-
increasing power consumption of AI clusters should continue to drive demand for higher
spec testing tools. Along with our forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit

demand in the coming three years (see our report), this should remain a major driver for
Chroma, in our view.

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 4: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 5: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 6: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

FT handlers to add another revenue stream
In HPC testing, Chroma used to focus mostly on the SL T side, although it did unveil its first
HPC FT handler earlier this year and is now going through the qualification process. We
believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

Morgan Stanley Research 7
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 40x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 40x is justified by the 68% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

研 究  報 告                
樺漢(6414.TT) 
產業：電子–電腦及週邊設備
3Q26 營收季增 3%，4Q26 營收預估與 3Q26 相當 
 投資評價與建議 
未來營收成⻑端視北美建廠需求與AI ， 毛利率提升主要仰賴整併Kontron的綜
效：樺漢計畫拿下3億美元之實體AI市佔，工控物聯網成⻑將由邊緣AI帶動，
北美建廠需求使智慧工廠持續成⻑，毛利率則藉由產品組合調整、新服務模式
的滲透率與整併Kontron的綜效以向上提升。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26營收季增3%(年增48%) ， 智慧工廠3Q26營收季減2%(年增84%) ：3Q26
營收496.4億元季增3%(年增48%)符合預估值(496.5億元)，工業物聯網事業
群、智慧軟體與方案事業群及智慧工廠與廠務事業群，分別季增4%、9%及-
2%(年增44%、-9%及84%)，智慧軟體與方案事業群4Q26為政府及企業單位
結帳高峰，4Q26營收比重可望高於3Q26，有助提升毛利率。 
4Q26營收預估與3Q26相當，在手訂單達2,550億元以上： 4Q26營收預估與
3Q26相當，成⻑驅動主要來⾃AI應用擴展 （由邊緣運算走向智慧平台）、半導
體擴產（先進製程/封裝/AI基建） 、智慧製造升級（⾃動化/機器人/數位轉型）
及全球在地化供應鏈布局；2,550億元以上的在手訂單能見度已延伸至 2027
年，其中工控物聯網受惠邊緣AI與交通/網絡設備強勁需求，智慧廠務工程持
續獲益於台灣及全球半導體大廠擴產與高科技廠務需求 ， 智慧軟體與方案則提
供高毛利且穩定的經常性收入。 
 投資風險 
整併效益不彰、實體AI需求不如預期。

估值(496.5億元)，工業物聯網事業群、智慧軟體與方案事業群及智慧工廠與廠務事業群，分別季增4%、9%及-2%(年增
44%、-9%及84%)，智慧軟體與方案事業群4Q26為政府及企業單位結帳高峰，4Q26營收比重可望高於3Q26，有助提
升毛利率；面對記憶體與原物料價格波動，樺漢已導入動態內部報價機制，並採取「鎖量、鎖價」策略，同時整合採購
與研發以開發替代方案，確保成本可控並維持毛利水準。 
樺漢三大事業群

### Page 3

3 
且穩定的經常性收入。 
樺漢看好實體AI：過去幾年全球聚焦於生成式AI，讓AI進入大眾生活與文字對話，樺漢認為生成式AI僅是起點，下⼀波
產業革命的關鍵在於實體AI，將讓AI開始進入真實世界，具備『看得見、聽得懂、想得通、做得到』的感知、理解、決
策與⾃主行動能力；根據Next Move Strategy Consulting預估，全球實體AI市場規模將由2025年的58 億美元成⻑至
2035年的1,200 億美元(CAGR35%)，10年預估成⻑將超過20倍；目前樺漢已全球部署超過100萬套系統，包含
Casino/Gaming (7.5萬/21萬套)、Lottery (6萬套)、ATM (25萬套)及POS/Self-Checkout (35萬套)，實體節點分布極為
廣泛；樺漢已打造⼀套共通核⼼技術平台，涵蓋Compute Platform (SOM/Edge)、Sensor Suite (Camera/LiDAR/IMU)
及Drive & Actuator、Connectivity (5G/Wi-Fi/TSN)，同⼀套平台可靈活⽀援 AMR（⾃主 移動機器人）、Mobile 
Manipulator （移動操作機器人）、Humanoid （人形機器人） 及Industrial Robot （工業機器人），大幅降低研發成本，
加速產線規模化複製落地 ； 樺漢機器人團隊已完成核⼼技術平台整合 。 早期 AMR 與工業⾃動化機器人已在智慧工廠與
物流場域大量量產；針對具備感知能力的人形機器人 （Humanoid）與 Mobile Manipulator，公司透過 NVIDIA Isaac
數位雙生進行驗證，預計於2026年起陸續展開客戶PoC與場域導入，並配合 Kontron 歐美通路擴大推廣，目標在10年
內拿下3億美元之實體AI市佔。 
樺漢與Kontron的整合策略定位

資料來源 : 樺漢法說會資料 
Kontron的EBITA/營收比率將由2026年的14%成⻑⾄2030年的16%：樺漢與Kontron之結合定位為 『One Platform， 
Two Strengths』，Kontron提供高階研發、KontronOS資安軟體與歐美品牌優勢，樺漢則提供全球規模化製造、供應
鏈採購與ODM執行能力，整合效益預期涵蓋四大構面 : 1、技術與產品整合，將KontronOS與5G/NAD/資安技術導入
樺漢硬體平台，拓展Edge AI / Physical AI方案，2、全球市場整合，實現亞洲、歐洲、北美區域交叉銷售與品牌聯合開
發，3、供應鏈整合，結合鴻海全球採購規模進行零組件採購優化，改善缺料及逾期積壓訂單，4、全球製造整合，執行
在地化製造策略，結合歐洲（捷克/保加利亞/匈牙利）、亞洲樺漢製造與北美區域生產；Kontron的2030年營收目標將
由2026年的16億歐元成⻑至2030年的26億歐元，EBITDA獲利目標將由2026年的2.25億歐元成⻑至2030年的4. 2億歐
元，EBITA/營收比率則將由 2026年的14% 成⻑至2030年的16%，軟 體解決方案收入將由 2026年的5.5億歐元倍增至 
2030年的10億歐元，核⼼成⻑動能包括鐵路通訊、國防航太及5G專網與關鍵連通技術；樺漢對Kontron持股已提高至
49.4% ， 總投資金額約2.87億歐元 ， 整合效應目標為EPS年貢獻2027年起達5元(獲利貢獻預估將佔稅前淨利的16%)以上 。

### Page 4

4 
樺漢與Kontron的整合策略定位

資料來源 : 樺漢法說會資料

| 樺漢的定位與優勢 
樺漢是鴻海艦隊的工業電腦旗艦：樺漢科技（6414）是鴻海集團旗下的重要工業電腦（IPC）與工業物聯網廠，是鴻海
艦隊的工業電腦旗艦。鴻海透過旗下投資公司共持有樺漢約24%的股權，為其最大股東與⺟集團，雙方在資源、供應鏈
及高科技AI智慧製造上緊密合作。樺漢的產品應用領域非常多元，包括智慧零售、智慧監控、智慧家居、資訊安全、交
通、製造、能源、醫療、建築、園區等，且透過整合硬體、軟體與人工智慧驅動服務積極推進ESaaS(樺漢解決方案即服
務)計畫，以滿足客戶多樣化需求。 
三大事業群架構分別為工業物聯網、智慧軟體與方案及智慧工廠與廠務：樺漢的三大事業群架構分別為工業物聯網事業
群、智慧軟體與方案事業群及智慧工廠與廠務事業群；工業物聯網事業群包括邊緣運算、網安通訊、工業與嵌入式系統
整合 。 智慧軟體與方案事業群著重高毛利軟硬雲整合 ， 涵蓋工業軟體 、 機器學習 、 數位雙生 、 雲地平台系統整合 ， Kontron 
在此板塊扮演關鍵的歐洲市場與軟體加值核⼼。智慧工廠與廠務事業群提供半導體設備及廠務系統、智能系統整合、高
科技材料與設備，由帆宣(6196)主導。 
併購帶來營收規模快速成⻑，三大事業群各有所⻑：樺漢透過併購壯大規模，營收由2015年的111億元僅短短十年就快
速10倍成⻑至2025年的1,423億元 ， 尤其入股Kontron(8/20後樺漢持股49.4%)與帆宣更是關鍵 。 樺漢看中Kontron的品
牌加持與歐洲的高市佔率，目前軌道交通產品Kontron已掌握歐洲逾50%市佔，並著手把相關方案導入亞太市場。樺漢
和Kontron在技術合作方面，建立共享平台，全面整合研發、製造、供應鏈、IT和人才等資源，協同設計產品，建構具
有全球競爭力的製造系統；產品整合則共享全球品牌資源，提升市場認知度與價值定位，並深入推廣產品線至亞太、歐
洲、北美等全球市場中⼼；市場聚焦方面，結合Kontron在基礎作業系統(Kontron OS)、物聯網軟體和5G網通方面的軟

### Page 5

5 
實力，以及樺漢在邊緣AI伺服器、機器人和強固型工業電腦方面的硬體優勢，共同打造實體AI技術平台。 
樺漢併購帶來營收規模快速成⻑

資料來源 : CMoney，玉山投顧整理、繪製 
實體 AI 帶來新⼀輪商機，樺漢拚五年內全球工業電腦第⼀名：實體 AI 的應用廣泛，如⾃動搬運叉車可應用於倉儲及
工廠內物料搬運，具備即時導航與避障，可提升效率並降低人力依賴。服務型機器人則可應用於清潔、導引及巡檢，
具有穩定感知與定位，可適用商辦、醫院、公共空間。樺漢認為，生成式 AI 只是開始。下⼀個十年真正改變世界的，
不只是 AI 會回答問題，而是 AI 開始進入真實世界。逐漸開始看得見、聽得懂、想得通、做得到。實體 AI 的定義應該
是讓 AI 具備感知、理解、決策與行動能力。樺漢預估 2035 年的實體 AI 市場規模可達 1,200 億美元，樺漢欲藉此商
機於五年內攻佔全球工業電腦第⼀名。樺漢目前已透過旗下全資⼦公司超恩，強化邊緣運算產品線，並結合 NVIDIA
的 Nemotron 模型，處理影像、聲音與文字等多模態資料。這類架構把資料分析留在終端端點執行，對應即時反應與
隱私要求較高的場域，也減少雲端端 AI 運算負擔。樺漢同步延伸 ESaaS(Ennoconn Solution as a Service，樺漢即服
務)服務內容，將硬體、軟體與人工智慧驅動服務整合到同⼀套方案中。樺漢 AI 聚焦在應用層與電力能源層，應用在智
慧製造、智慧城市、ESG 以及發展利基小語言模型。

| 工業電腦產業簡介 
工業電腦對環境的適應力高於個人電腦：1984年，IBM發表了5531型工業電腦，隨後於1985年推出基於Intel 80286處
理器的IBM7531，正式開啟工業電腦新紀元。工業電腦（IPC）發展至今已有40多年的歷史，從最初只負責簡單的流程
監測，進化到現今具備高效能算力的邊緣控制中⼼，其最大的特⾊在於使用「工業級用料」，能夠保障⻑達10至 15年
的使用需求，並且能克服各式嚴苛的環境挑戰，確保設備能持續穩定地運作。工業電腦最顯著的特性便是其針對惡劣環
境的耐用性，為了應對容易有大量粉塵的環境，多數的機型都採用「無風扇」和「⼀體成形」的設計，避免風扇故障引
起的熱當機或灰塵吸入造成的短路；此外，為因應戶外作業或極端氣候，大部分的工業電腦都會通過寬溫(例如：可運行
的溫度區間為 -40°C～85°C)與寬壓(例如：可輸入電壓區間為 9V～36V)測試，並搭載防塵、防水、防震、耐衝擊的機
制，確保設備在震動頻繁的環境下仍能穩定運作，工業電腦要具備嚴苛環境的適應力，高可靠度，擁有豐富的工業接口
及較⻑的生命週期管理。 
工業電腦與⼀般電腦的差異

資料來源 : Google、 玉山投顧整理、編製

### Page 7

7 
工作溫度 、 防護等級等為評定工業電腦優劣的重要指標 ， 以冷卻機制來說 ， 個人電腦依賴主動散熱 ， 灰塵與故障風險高 ，
工業電腦則採被動、密封設計，降低⻑期失效機率；擴充與客製化方面，工業電腦可高度客製，對應工業、醫療、車載
等垂直場域需求，而非僅僅只有通用規格；電源管理能力方面，工業電腦具備抗突波與點火控制，能在不穩定供電下持
續運作；至於作業系統穩定性方面，工業電腦多採用⻑期⽀援版本，可避免更新中斷營運。 
工業電腦優劣的重要指標

資料來源 : Google、 玉山投顧整理、編製  
工業電腦的應用場域多元化：隨著技術的演進，工業電腦的應用場景早已跨越工廠的圍牆，在各個專業領域的解決方案
成功落地，從車載應用、軌道交通、船舶海事、無人載具、人型機器人、工業機器人、工業⾃動化、5G通訊網路到邊境
安防，工業電腦的應用場域多元化，隨處可見。 
工業電腦的分類與應用場景

資料來源 : Google，玉山投顧整理、編製 
⾃從工業電腦從工廠走進我們生活日常的POS、交通售票系統、醫療診斷到博弈娛樂等，它的重要性就日益加重，舉例
來說 ， 於智慧醫療其應用於影像診斷與手術輔助 ， 核⼼在於精準度與醫療級電氣安全 ， 錯誤的成本極高 ； 智慧交通方面 ，
車載與軌道監控需⻑時間運算與抗環境⼲擾，任何中斷都直接影響公共安全。 
全球工業電腦市場2035年將達104億美元，工業電腦台廠各有專精：根據研調機構構Research Nester統計與預估，全
球工業電腦市場規模將由2025年的61億美元，成⻑至2035年的104億美元，年均複合成⻑率為7.9%，台廠從上游半導
體至下游電⼦組裝產業供應鏈完整，具上下游垂直整合優勢且各有專精。

台廠具上下游垂直整合優勢

資料來源 : Google，玉山投顧整理  
AI時代帶來邊緣運算需求 
AI時代到來，工業電腦將從硬體代工走向邊緣運算平台化，軟體的整合能力將決定產品毛利率的高低，工業電腦也從第
⼀代的控制功能跨步到第⼆代的連網功能，再進而邁向大步往第三代的人工智慧及時推理功能前進。未來，工業電腦已
不再只是單純的控制設備，而是逐步成為集資料蒐集、即時運算與通訊於⼀⾝的邊緣中樞；定位於邊緣運算讓工業電腦
不再只是資料收集設備，而是真正具備「看得見、聽得懂、想得出判斷」的能力，能在工廠端即時完成視覺檢測與設備
健康判讀；更關鍵的是，這種結合正在實際解決⻑期困擾製造業的問題，那就是「經驗無法複製」，透過 AI 學習資深
工匠的判斷邏輯，工業電腦得以把隱性知識轉為可執行的數位規則，即使在技術人力短缺的情況下，生產品質依然能被
穩定維持。 
工業電腦的世代演進

資料來源 : Google，玉山投顧整理、編製 
⾃從NVIDIA執行⻑黃仁勳宣告 「每⼀家工業公司都將成為機器人公司」，並將實體AI定義為繼生成式AI之後的下⼀波科
技浪潮後，AI便不再只是雲端的虛擬智慧，而是能感知環境、理解物理規律、並在真實世界採取行動的能力，這句話所
指向的核⼼硬體，正是工業電腦。未來工業電腦由於內建NPU、GPU等AI加速模組，將可在本地端即時分析影像、震動
等高頻資料，避免等待回傳結果，具有即時推論的效能；另外，也具備低延遲響應且降低頻寬需求，再加上模型的持續
優化，讓工業電腦扮演邊緣AI推論平台，除了整合機器視覺系統的算力核⼼，同時也協作機器人與人形機器人的控制大
腦。邊緣運算正在讓工業電腦從「事後記錄者」轉變為「即時預警者」，工業電腦不再只是設備，而會成為對抗全球勞
動力短缺、突破職人經驗瓶頸的關鍵工具，推動智慧製造從「改善」走向真正的「質變」，預估全球邊緣運算的市場規
模於2033年將可突破千億美元，年均複合成⻑率將可達21%。

工業電腦(IPC)3.0於2026年後將進入主體AI時代

資料來源 : 樺漢法說會資料 
工業電腦歷經成⻑及轉型升級 ， IPC 1.0 (2011至2015年)是工業電腦時代 ， 主要定義讓設備具備運算能力 。 IPC 2.0 (2016
至2020年)是工業物聯網時代，主要是讓設備彼此連結。而IPC 3.0 (2020至2025年)為智慧平台時代，目地是讓數據產生
價值。2026年之後進入主體AI時代，將讓AI從數據分析走向實際執行，從協助決策走向⾃主行動。

 절대 (달러환산) 0.3  (11.5) 239.5

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

spike feed acyclical inflation.

### Page 4

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

A) The Yen Regains Some Strength. B) 5-Year Forward Yields Hold Above 5%.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.
Exhibit 4: U.S. Exceptionalism: Record Inflows of Portfolio and FDI Inflows.
A) Net Foreign Purchases of US Equities and Investment Funds. B) FDI Inflows to the U.S.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

decision on Wednesday. Headline inﬂation has been running above
the NBP's target of 2.5% +/- 1 percentage point in recent months.
However, with little evidence of broader price pressures building, the
Monetary Policy Council still appears to have time before it needs to
respond.

staﬀ's new macroeconomic projection. If it points to inﬂation

Hungary: Industrial output and retail sales
data ahead
September industrial output data is released on Wednesday, while
Friday brings September retail sales data. Some payback looks
likely in industrial production after two consecutive months of
strong growth, but this should not derail the generally positive
trend. Growth remains concentrated among manufacturers

bringing new capacities online.
Meanwhile, after the surprisingly weak August reading, retail sales
should regain momentum in September, with food and non-food
sales recovering, partly oﬀset by the drag from rising fuel prices.
Czech Republic: CNB expected to raise
rates by 25bp
The CNB is expected to raise rates by 25bp to 4.00% on Thursday
as inﬂation pressures continue to build. Headline and core inﬂation
have exceeded the central bank’s forecasts, while higher energy

and food prices and accelerating wages point to further upside
risks. The CNB’s new forecast should reﬂect substantially higher
assumptions for oil, gas and eurozone inﬂation, alongside stronger
public-sector and minimum-wage growth. We expect GDP growth
of 1.8% this year and 2.3% in 2027, below the CNB’s current
projections.

At the same time, inﬂation is expected to accelerate to 3.9% in

Turkey: Monthly inﬂation expected to rise
on energy pressures

Tuesday brings October CPI data. We expect monthly inﬂation of
2.4%, driven by higher energy pressures, which the CBT has
highlighted as an upside risk, as well as seasonal increases in food
and clothing prices. This would bring annual inﬂation down slightly,
from 29.7% in September to 29.6%.
CCA: Armenia and Uzbekistan central banks
expected to stay on hold
We expect the Central Bank of Armenia to keep its policy rate
unchanged at 6.75% next Tuesday. The case against another hike

is underpinned by the moderation in headline inﬂation to 4.3% YoY
in September from 4.4% in August, alongside the continued
strength of the dram.
Still, inﬂation remains above the CBA's 3% target, while Armenia's
exposure to external risks amid a relatively low real rate means that
a further nominal rate hike cannot be ruled out over the medium
term. We therefore expect the CBA to maintain cautious guidance,
keeping the door open to renewed tightening should inﬂationary

pressures strengthen.
The Central Bank of Uzbekistan postponed its 28 October meeting
to 4 November to incorporate updated 3Q data into its forecasts.
Historically, such delays have not signalled an imminent policy
move, with the previous postponements in 2024 and 2025
eventually followed by holds. Little has changed since last week’s
preview, and we continue to expect the policy rate to remain at
14.00% as a base-case scenario.

## Document 312: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

## Document 418: The Institute Employment Report_ September 2026 Bank of America Institute analysis shows slower payroll growth, declining unemployment payments and continued strength in lower-inco

Economy 
The Institute Employment Report: September 2026 
07 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Job growth softened further in September, according to Bank of America's deposit data, with growth of 1.4% year-over-year 
(YoY), from 1.5% in August. But falling unemployment payments in September indicate a relatively healthy overall picture. 
• Lower-income households' wage growth remains strongest, despite a narrowing gap. After-tax wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY 
for lower-income households in September, while rising to 3.8% and 3.7% for middle- and higher-income households, 
respectively. 
• Lower tax withholdings and a relative strengthening in the younger/lower-income end of the labor market are driving this story. 
Additionally, average hours have risen in some lower-paid industries, which is also boosting pay packets.

Job growth slowed in September, but unemployment trends are benign  
Our estimate of job growth – which uses Bank of America deposit data to track changes in the number of customer accounts 
receiving a paycheck (see Methodology) – slowed further in September, to 1.4% year-over-year (YoY) from 1.5% YoY in August. 
The data can be noisy, partly due to seasonal variation and differences in pay-period timing, but in our view, the latest reading is 
consistent with the easing in payrolls growth seen in the September Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) data (Exhibit 1). 
Still, despite slower hiring, there is little sign of a rise in joblessness in Bank of America data. Unemployment payments into 
customer accounts showed a YoY decline in September (Exhibit 2). So, overall, labor market trends appear resilient.

After-tax wage growth cooled for lower-income, but rose for other cohorts

pattern we observed over much of 2025 and 2026 (Exhibit 3).

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Estimate of payroll growth from Bank of America internal data is based on the change in customer accounts receiving a paycheck in the month. 
An adjustment is made for the difference between overall population growth and customer account growth. Occasionally historic payments can 
be reclassified, leading to revisions in the series.  
An estimate of bonus growth from Bank of America deposit data is calculated by looking at customers who have received an inbound ACH 
payroll transaction in the last two years. From this sample an estimate of bonuses is derived by looking for payroll transactions which are over 
50% higher than the median regular payroll payments received by the customer. Of these payments only those that were received around the 
same time in each of the last two years are selected.  
The job-to-job change rate (j2j rate) is defined as the proportion of customers with an identified change in their employer as a proportion of 
the total number of customers with employment income. We estimate the median pay rise associated with a j2j change using the pay in the 
first three months of the new job compared to the same three months a year ago. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

CPB PUBLICATIE – National Productivity Monitor Netherlands   Page 2 of 27 
Preface 
Productivity has shifted to the top of the political agenda in the last years. In view of the limitations to a 
further expansion of the labour supply, productivity growth is the principal way to support our future living 
standard. Unfortunately, the Netherlands, as all OECD countries, has been struggling with a declining trend in 
productivity growth during the last decades. We do not yet fully understand the causes of this global 
productivity slowdown.

CPB is part of a network of independent National Productivity Boar ds in the EU. The CPB has been a 
National Productivity Board since 2017. The main NPB tasks are monitoring and analysing productivity 
developments in the Netherlands. As in previous years, we summarise research on productivity issues in this 
Annual Report 2024.

We are preparing three publications this year. First, we participate in a multi-country project of the 
Productivity Institute (UK). The aim of this project is to identify and compare country -specific productivity 
trends and pro-productivity policies. Second, we will analyse the evidence on changing bus iness dynamics in 
the Netherlands and the consequences for firm-level productivity and innovation. Finally, we will provide the 
analysis underlying our projections of long run productivity.

Pieter Hasekamp 
Director CPB

### Page 3

CPB PUBLICATIE – National Productivity Monitor Netherlands   Page 3 of 27 
Samenvatting 
De arbeidsproductiviteit in de marktsector daalde in 2023 met 1,4%. Sinds 2000 is de productiviteit alleen 
in 2009 nog sterker gedaald. De uitfasering van de gaswinning sinds 2014 heeft de jaarlijkse productiviteits -
groei met gemiddeld 0,3%-punt gedrukt.

We begrijpen nog niet helemaal waarom de productiviteitsgroei is afgenomen. Zoals in alle OESO-landen 
heeft Nederland last van een vertraging van de productiviteitsgroei. In een literatuuronderzoek vinden we 
verschillende verklaringen, maar de bijdrage van elke oorzaak is moeilijk te bepalen vanwege de sterke 
verbanden tussen de oorzaken.

Het productiviteitsverschil tussen de 10% meest productieve bedrijven en minder productieve bedrijven 
nam in 2021 toe. We stelden in ons vorige rapport (CPB, 2024) vast dat de productiviteitskloof tussen de 
bedrijven van de top 10% en de bedrijven van de onderste 10% sterk is toegenomen  in 2020. Uit een update van 
deze analyse blijkt dat de kloof in 2021 groot bleef en niet te wijten was aan de bijzondere omstandigheden in 
2020. Productiviteitsgroei in de economie kan voortkomen uit productiviteitsgroei binnen bestaande 
bedrijven of door activiteiten te verschuiven naar productievere bedrijven. De bijdrage van de verschuiving 
daalde in de verwerkende industrie en de dienstensector in de periode 2011-2019. De bijdrage van de 
bedrijvendynamiek bleef stabiel in de handel, terwijl deze belangrijker werd voor overige industrieën (bouw, 
energie, water en afval).

Exporteurs en hun directe toeleveranciers vormen de meest productieve groep bedrijven in Nederland.  
Uit de literatuur weten we dat exporterende bedrijven productiever zijn dan niet -exporterende bedrijven. We 
laten met nieuwe netwerkdata een duidelijk patroon zien: exporteurs zijn groter en productiever dan hun 
toeleveranciers, die op hun beurt beter presteren dan hun eigen toeleveranciers. De productiviteit van 
exporteurs en hun toeleveranciers wordt versterkt via drie mechanismen: leere ffecten, selectie en 
schaalvoordelen. Beleid dat bedrijfsnetwerken versterkt, kan bijdragen aan productiviteitsgroei.

### Page 4

CPB PUBLICATIE – National Productivity Monitor Netherlands   Page 4 of 27 
Summary

Labour productivity in the market sector declined by 1.4% in 2023. Since 2000, only 2009 saw a stronger fall 
in  productivity.. Phasing out gas extraction since 2014 depressed annual productivity growth by 0.3%-point on 
average.

We do not fully understand why the productivity growth has decreased. As in all OECD countries, the 
Netherlands is suffering from a slowdown in productivity growth. A survey of the literature provides various 
explanations, but the contribution of each cause is difficult to determine due to the strong links between the 
causes.

The productivity difference between frontier firms and non -frontier firms was growing in 2021. We 
observed that the productivity gap between the top-10% firms and the bottom-10% firms strongly increased in 
2020. An update of this analysis shows that the gap remained large in 2021. Productivity growth can originate 
from growth within existing firms or by reallocating activities to more productive firms. The contribution of 
reallocation declined in manufacturing and services during the period 2011 -2019. The contribution of business 
dynamics remained stable in trade while it became more important for other industries (construction, energy, 
wate and waste).

Exporters and their direct suppliers form the most productive group of firms in the Netherlands. From 
previous research we know that exporting firms are more productive than non-exporting firms. From new 
network data, a clear pattern emerges: exporters are larger and more productive than their suppliers, who, in 
turn, outperform their own suppliers. Productivity of exporters and their suppliers benefit through three main 
channels: learning effects, selection and economies of scale. Policies that strengthen firm networks can 
contribute to fostering productivity growth.

### Page 5

CPB PUBLICATIE – National Productivity Monitor Netherlands   Page 5 of 27 
1 Introduction 
This Annual Report summarises research on productivity developments in the Netherlands. 1 It consists of four 
parts.

In the next section we summarize the unfavourable productivity developments in 2023. We show that phasing 
out gas extraction since 2014 contributed to the low productivity growth rates in the Netherlands compared to 
other European countries.

Various explanations are found in the literature why productivity growth is slowing down. No consensus is yet 
reached on the contribution of each explanation of the slowdown. We discuss the main studies in the third 
section.

The fourth section, provided by Michael Polder (Statistics Netherlands), updates and extends the evidence on 
firm-level productivity. He documents productivity differences between firms and shows that the contribution 
of continuing firms, new firms and closing firms to productivity growth differ across industries, as well as 
between time periods.

The last section exploits new data on the Dutch production networks. 2 It is well known that exporting firms are 
more productive than an average firm. This section discusses the impact on productivity of firms that are 
suppliers to exporting firms.

1 Our Annual Report of 2023 can be found at annual report 2023. The reports of the other NPBs are collected at this EC-website. 
2 This is a translation of Freeman et al. (2024).

### Page 6

CPB PUBLICATIE – National Productivity Monitor Netherlands   Page 6 of 27 
2 Recent productivity developments 
Labour productivity in the market sector in 2023 was 1.4% lower than in 2022. Labour productivity is 
defined as value added per hour worked. 3 Figure 2.1a shows that productivity only declined more (with -2.2%) 
during the great recession in 2009 (CBS, 2024). The decomposition in figure 2.1b shows that the negative 
labour productivity growth in 2023 was driven by negative contributions of TFP growth (-0.8%) and capital 
deepening (-0.8%).4 The number of working hours has increased, while the capital stock reduced (due to non -
ICT capital). The long term decline in capital stock per hour since the mid 2010 -s seems to continue at an even 
faster pace. TFP growth (a measure of the efficiency with which labour and capital are used in the economy) 
turned negative in 2023. TFP grew fast in 2021 and 2022, in part through recovery from the corona pandemic 
shock in 2020

Figure 2.1 Labour productivity in de market sector declined in 2023

Source: CBS (2024); 2022 & 2023 are preliminary.

Phasing out gas extraction depressed productivity growth, starting in 2014. The mining and quarrying 
industry is very capital intensive, resulting in a high labour productivity. Labour productivity in this industry 
grew on average with 0.7% before 2014, while it fell with almost 16% per year after 2014  due to the phasing out 
of gas extraction (De Vries et al., 2024). Figure 2.2 shows that the average labour productivity growth in the 
Netherlands was one of the lowest in Europe. However, whereas the average contribution of the mining 
industry was negligible in most of the countries, it was negative ( -0.3%-point) in the Netherlands (and +0.2% 
in the US). The productivity growth of the market sector excluding mining in the Netherlands was much closer 
to the average value in Europe. Next to mining, other industries that contributed negatively to productivity 
growth in 2023 are transport and storage (-0.5%-point) and manufacturing (-0.4%-point).

3 The market sector includes all sectors, with the exception of the government, education, households and rental & trading of real 
estate.  
4 We use the identity: 𝑌̃𝑡 − 𝐻̃𝑡 = 𝛼𝑡(𝐾̃𝑡 − 𝐻̃𝑡) + (1 − 𝛼𝑡)𝐶̃𝑡 + 𝐴̃𝑡, where 𝑌̃ is value added growth, 𝐻̃ is growth in hours worked, 𝐾̃ is 
capital growth, 𝐶̃ is labour composition growth, 𝐴̃ is TFP  growth and 𝛼 is the share of capital costs.
